\lVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\bm{\Delta}}}\rVert\leq\frac{\lVert\mathbf{A}-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}\rVert}{\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{A})\sigma_{\min}(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}})}\leq\frac{\alpha(1-\eta){\rm e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha\kappa_{1}}}{\lambda\eta^{2}},

where we have used the lower bound
2\lambda\eta
for
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{A}_{2})
and
\sigma_{\min}(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}})
. This lower bound is obtained from the ridge part of the regularizer.
Define

\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha):=\frac{\alpha(1-\eta){\rm e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha\kappa_{1}}}{\lambda\eta^{2}}.

(108)

As we will discuss later, we will ensure that
\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha)\rightarrow 0
as
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
. Hence,
\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\bm{\Delta}}}\|
will be small too. Using this result and the triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\Big(\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1}-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\Big)\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}|

(109)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\bm{\Delta}}}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}|+|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\bm{\Delta}}}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\rVert^{2}\lVert\mathbf{B}\rVert^{2}\lVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\bm{\Delta}}}\rVert\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{A})}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\rVert^{2}\lVert\mathbf{B}\rVert^{2}\lVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\bm{\Delta}}}\rVert\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{A})}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\rVert^{2}\lVert\mathbf{B}\rVert^{2}\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha)\left(\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\right).

Similar to the proof of (
5.4
), it is not hard to see that

\lVert\mathbf{B}_{2}\rVert\leq\omega_{s}

under event
{\cal A}_{L}
. Furthermore, given the scaling we have considered in our paper, as will be clarified later,
\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|=O_{p}(1)
. Hence, the term
\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\rVert^{2}\lVert\mathbf{B}\rVert^{2}\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha)(\frac{1}{\lambda\eta})
will go to zero with high probability as
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
.

In the rest of our proof, we will work with
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}
. Still there are two sources of dependency between the middle matrix and
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}
: (1) the input argument of
\ddot{\ell}
, and (2) The dependence of
{\cal B}_{1,+}
and
{\cal F}
on
\mathbf{x}_{i}
. In order to remove the dependence of
{\cal F}
and
{\cal B}_{1,+}
, we lift the problem to a higher dimensional problem for the reasons that will become clearer later. Define the two sets:

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{+}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\cal B}_{1,+}\cup{\cal B}_{0},

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{-}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\cal F}\setminus{\cal B}_{1,+}.

(110)

Use these two sets to define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle=~{\rm diag}\left[\lambda\left\{\ddot{r}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right\}_{{\cal B}^{+}}\right]+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle=~\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{-}},

As will be clarified later, working with
{\cal B}^{+}
and
{\cal B}^{-}
will be helpful when we would like to remove the dependancies that are caused by
{\cal B}^{+}
and
{\cal B}^{-}
. Hence, as the first step our aim is to obtain an upper bound on the difference

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}-\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|.

We write
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{E}&\mathbf{F}\\
\mathbf{F}^{\top}&\mathbf{G}\end{pmatrix},

(111)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\rm diag}\left[\lambda\left\{\ddot{r}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right\}_{{\cal B}_{1,+}}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}_{1,+}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}_{0}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm diag}\left[\lambda\left\{\ddot{r}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right\}_{{\cal B}_{0}}\right]+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}_{0}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}_{0}}.

Similar to the proof of (
5.4
) it can be checked that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}\|_{2}\leq\omega^{s}

(112)

with probability at least
1-q_{n}
.
Using the matrix inversion of block matrices (Lemma
11
) we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{E}^{-1}+\mathbf{E}^{-1}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{-1}&-\mathbf{E}^{-1}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\\
-\mathbf{H}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{-1}&\mathbf{H}\end{pmatrix},

(113)

where

\mathbf{H}=(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{-1}\mathbf{F})^{-1}.

Furthermore, similar to the derivation in (
73
) we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{H}\|=

\displaystyle~(\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{-1}\mathbf{F}))^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\rho(\alpha)-\frac{(\omega^{s})^{2}}{2\lambda\eta}},

(114)

with probability
1-q_{n}
. Both
\rho(\alpha)
and
\omega^{s}
are defined at the beginning of the proof. It follows from the definition of
\rho(\alpha)
that
\frac{1}{\rho(\alpha)-\frac{(\omega^{s})^{2}}{2\lambda\eta}}
goes to zero for large values of
\alpha
and hence the norm of
\mathbf{H}
should be considered as a small number. If we define

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}:=\begin{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}&\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{1}\times{\cal B}_{0}|}\\
\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{0}\times{\cal B}_{1}|}&\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{0}\times{\cal B}_{0}|}\end{pmatrix},

(115)

and

\bm{\Delta}:=\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}-\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1},

then it is straightforward to show that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}

\displaystyle-~\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle-\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle+2|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}\|\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+2\|\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\|\right).

(116)

To find an upper bound for (
5.6
) we have to bound
\|\bm{\Delta}\|
,
\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\|
and
\|\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\|
. Similar to our previous calculations, we have under event
{\cal A}_{L}
that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\omega^{s},

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mathbf{A}}_{2}^{\dagger}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}_{2})}\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda\eta}.

(117)

Finally, by using Weyl’s theorem (Lemma
9
), we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}\|\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{E}^{-1}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{-1}&\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{1}\times{\cal B}_{0}|}\\
\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{0}\times{\cal B}_{1}|}&\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{0}\times{\cal B}_{0}|})\\
\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{1}\times{\cal B}_{1}|}&\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{1}\times{\cal B}_{0}|}\\
\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{0}\times{\cal B}_{1}|}&\mathbf{H}\\
\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{1}\times{\cal B}_{1}|}&\mathbf{E}^{-1}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{H}\\
\mathbf{H}^{\top}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{-1}&\mathbf{0}_{|{\cal B}_{0}\times{\cal B}_{0}|})\\
\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{\|\mathbf{H}\|\|\mathbf{F}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\min}(\mathbf{E})}+\|\mathbf{H}\|+2\frac{\|\mathbf{F}\|\|\mathbf{H}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{E})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\rho(\alpha)-\frac{(\omega^{s})^{2}}{2\lambda\eta}}\left(1+\frac{\omega^{s}}{2\lambda\eta}+\frac{(\omega^{s})^{2}}{(2\lambda\eta)^{2}}\right),

(118)

under event
{\cal A}_{L}
. To obtain the last inequality we have used (
112
), and (
5.6
). Define

\gamma_{1}^{s}(\alpha):=\frac{1}{\rho(\alpha)-\frac{(\omega^{s})^{2}}{2\lambda\eta}}\left(1+\frac{\omega^{s}}{2\lambda\eta}+\frac{(\omega^{s})^{2}}{(2\lambda\eta)^{2}}\right).

(119)

By combining (
5.6
), (
5.6
) and (
5.6
) we conclude that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}

(120)

\displaystyle-~\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}\|\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+2\|\bar{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}(\omega^{s})^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}\|(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}),

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}(\omega^{s})^{2}\gamma_{1}^{s}(\alpha)\left(\gamma_{1}^{s}(\alpha)+\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\right),

under event
{\cal A}_{L}
.

As discussed before, we eventually show that as
\alpha\rightarrow\infty
,
\gamma_{1}^{s}(\alpha)\rightarrow 0
and hence the upper bound in (
120
) will go to zero.
Hence, in the rest of the proof, we aim to show that
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
is small.

In the next step we would like to remove the dependence of the input argument of
\ddot{\ell}
on
\mathbf{x}_{i}
. Define,

\displaystyle\check{\mathbf{A}}

\displaystyle:={\rm diag}\left[\lambda\left\{{\ddot{r}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha})\right\}\right]+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}},

\displaystyle\check{\mathbf{B}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{-}}.

Define

\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}:={\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})\right]-{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha})\right]

We have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{A}}=\check{\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}

According to Lemma
12
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}-\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}

\displaystyle+\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}

(121)

Also, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{B}}-\check{\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{-}}.

(122)

Define
\bm{\delta}_{B}=\tilde{\mathbf{B}}-\check{\mathbf{B}}
and
\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}=\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}-\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\delta}_{B}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{B}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\check{\mathbf{B}}\check{\mathbf{B}}_{2}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\check{\mathbf{B}}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}.

(123)

Our goal is to show that each of the terms in (
5.6
) are small for large values of
n,p
. The two terms
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
and
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\check{\mathbf{B}}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
can be bounded in very similar ways and will have similar upper bounds. Also, the two terms

\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\delta}_{B}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
and
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\bm{\delta}_{B}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
can be handled in similar fashion and will have similar upper bounds.
Hence, we only study the following three terms:

1.

Finding an upper bound for
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\check{\mathbf{B}}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
:

Note that if it were not for the dependance of
{\cal B}^{+}
on
\mathbf{x}_{i}
we could claim that
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
is independent of
\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\check{\mathbf{B}}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}
, and we could use the Hanson-Wright inequality (Lemma
15
). So, in order to remove the dependence to be able to use the Hanson-Wright inequality, we will use union bound on sets
{\cal B}^{+}
and
{\cal B}^{-}
in the following way. First note that
|{\cal B}^{+}|\geq p-d_{n}
and
|{\cal B}^{-}|\leq d_{n}
. Let
{\cal T}^{+}
and
{\cal T}^{-}
denote two fixed sets of size larger than
p-d_{n}
, and smaller than
d_{n}
(not dependent on
\mathbf{x}_{i}
) respectively. We define

\displaystyle\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}:=

\displaystyle~{\rm diag}\left[\lambda\left\{{\ddot{r}}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha})\right\}\right]+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}^{+}}.

\displaystyle\check{\check{\mathbf{B}}}=

\displaystyle~\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}^{-}}

(124)

For fixed
{\cal T}^{+}
and
{\cal T}^{-}
,
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}}
is independent of
\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}
and
\check{\check{\mathbf{B}}}
. Therefore if

\mathbf{G}=\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\check{\check{\mathbf{B}}}\check{\check{\mathbf{B}}}^{\top}\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1},

then from the Hanson-Wright inequality, Lemma
15
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}|\ \mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i})|>t\ |\ \mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2{\rm e}^{-c\Big(\frac{(p-d_{n})^{2}t^{2}}{\|\mathbf{G}\|_{HS}^{2}}\wedge\frac{(p-d_{n})t}{\|\mathbf{G}\|_{2}}\Big)}.\hskip 71.13188pt

(125)

To use this bound we have to calculate the three terms:
\|\mathbf{G}\|_{2}
,
\|\mathbf{G}\|_{HS}
, and

\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}|\ \mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i})
:

(a)

\|\mathbf{G}\|_{2}
:

It is straightforward to see that (see e.g. the derivation of (
5.4
))

\|\mathbf{G}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|\check{\check{\mathbf{B}}}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{\min}(\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}})}\leq\frac{\omega^{s}}{4\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}},

(126)

under event
{\cal A}_{L}
.

(b)

\|\mathbf{G}\|_{HS}^{2}
:

The rank of matrix
{\cal G}
is at most
d_{n}
(the maximum size of
{\cal T}^{-}
). Hence,

\|\mathbf{G}\|^{2}_{HS}\leq d_{n}\|\mathbf{G}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{d_{n}(\omega^{s})^{2}}{16\lambda^{4}\eta^{4}}

(127)

(c)

\mathbb{E}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}})
:

Let
\bm{\Sigma}_{{\cal T}}
the covariance matrix of
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}}
. We have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}|=\left|{\rm Tr}(\bm{\Sigma}_{{\cal T}}^{1/2}\mathbf{G}\bm{\Sigma}_{{\cal T}}^{1/2})\right|

(128)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle d_{n}\|\bm{\Sigma}_{{\cal T}}^{1/2}\mathbf{G}\bm{\Sigma}_{{\cal T}}^{1/2}\|\leq d_{n}\|\bm{\Sigma}_{{\cal T}}\|\|\mathbf{G}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{n}\rho_{\max}\|\mathbf{G}\|\leq\frac{d_{n}\rho_{\max}\omega^{s}}{4\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}},

with probability
1-q_{n}
. Here, to obtain Inequality
(a)
, we used the fact that the rank of
\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{G}\bm{\Sigma}^{1/2}
is less than or equal to the rank of
\mathbf{G}
which is less than or equal to
d_{n}
.

Furthermore, from Lemma
19
we have

\mathbb{P}(\sigma_{\max}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\geq(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max})\leq{\rm e}^{-p},

(129)

where
\rho_{\max}=\sigma_{\max}(\Sigma)
. Let the event
\cal{E}
denote the event
\sigma_{\max}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})<(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}
. We know that

\mathbb{P}({\cal E})>1-{\rm e}^{-p}.

We remind the reader that

\omega^{s}=\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\ddot{\ell}_{i}(t\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}+(1-t)\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha}.

Combining (
1
), (
126
), (
127
), (
128
), (
129
) and using the following definitions:

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}{4\eta^{2}\lambda^{2}}

(130)

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}>t+{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\mathbb{P}}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}>t+{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}},{\cal E})+{\mathbb{P}}({\cal E}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2{\rm e}^{-c\Big(\frac{(p-d_{n}))^{2}t^{2}}{d_{n}\gamma_{3}^{2}}\wedge\frac{(p-d_{n})t}{\gamma_{3}}\Big)}+{\rm e}^{-p}.

(131)

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}>t+d_{n}\gamma_{3})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2{\rm e}^{-c\Big(\frac{(p-d_{n})^{2}t^{2}}{d_{n}\gamma_{3}^{2}}\wedge\frac{(p-d_{n})t}{\gamma_{3}}\Big)}+{\rm e}^{-p}.

(132)

If we define the event
\tilde{{\cal E}}
as the event that (
10
) holds, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{\underset{|{\cal T}^{+}|\geq p-d_{n}}{{\cal T}^{+}}}\max_{\underset{|{\cal T}^{-}|\leq d_{n}}{{\cal T}^{-}}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}>t+d_{n}\gamma_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{\underset{|{\cal T}^{+}|\geq p-d_{n}}{{\cal T}^{+}}}\max_{\underset{|{\cal T}^{-}|\leq d_{n}}{{\cal T}^{-}}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}>t+d_{n}\gamma_{3}|{\cal E},\tilde{{\cal E}})+\mathbb{P}({\cal E})+\mathbb{P}(\tilde{{\cal E}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\underset{|{\cal T}^{+}|\geq p-d_{n}}{{\cal T}^{+}}}\sum_{\underset{|{\cal T}^{-}|\leq d_{n}}{{\cal T}^{-}}}\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}^{\top}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}^{+}}>t+d_{n}\gamma_{3}|{\cal E},\tilde{{\cal E}})+\mathbb{P}({\cal E})+\mathbb{P}(\tilde{{\cal E}})

\displaystyle\leq 2d_{n}{p\choose d_{n}}^{2}\left(2{\rm e}^{-c\Big(\frac{(p-d_{n})^{2}t^{2}}{d_{n}\gamma_{3}^{2}}\wedge\frac{(p-d_{n})t}{\gamma_{3}}\Big)}\right)+{\rm e}^{-p}+q_{n}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}{\rm e}^{4d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left(2{\rm e}^{-c\Big(\frac{(p-d_{n})^{2}t^{2}}{d_{n}\gamma_{3}^{2}}\wedge\frac{(p-d_{n})t}{\gamma_{3}}\Big)}\right)+{\rm e}^{-p}+q_{n},

(133)

where to obtain inequality (a) we used Stirling approximation together with the assumption
d_{n}<{\rm e}^{d_{n}\log(ep/d_{n})}
.
By setting
t=\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}d_{n}}{p-d_{n}}
we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\check{\mathbf{B}}\check{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}>\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{p-d_{n}}+d_{n}\gamma_{3})

\displaystyle\leq{\rm e}^{d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left(2{\rm e}^{-c\Big(\frac{d_{n}\log^{4}p}{\gamma_{3}^{2}}\wedge\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{\gamma_{3}}\Big)}\right)+q_{n}+{\rm e}^{-p}.

(134)

The probability in (
1
) is small for large values of
d_{n}
and
p
.

2.

Finding an upper bound for
\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
:

Define

{\check{\mathbf{v}}}:=\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}},

(135)

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{\top}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}

(136)

Using Lemma
12
we have

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle~|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle+|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~|\mathbf{u}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\check{\mathbf{v}}|+|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\check{\mathbf{v}}|

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\sum_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell,ii}^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{2}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{2}}+\sqrt{\sum_{i}\Delta_{\ell,ii}^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}^{2}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\sum_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell,ii}^{2}}(\sum_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{4})^{\frac{1}{4}}(\sum_{i}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{4})^{\frac{1}{4}}+\sqrt{\sum_{i}\Delta_{\ell,ii}^{2}}(\sum_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}^{4})^{\frac{1}{4}}(\sum_{i}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{4})^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\sum_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell,ii}^{2}}(\sum_{i}\mathbf{u}_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\max_{i}|\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\sum_{i}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}+\sqrt{\sum_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell,ii}^{2}}(\sum_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\max_{i}|\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\sum_{i}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}.

(137)

Note that since
\sigma_{\min}(\tilde{\mathbf{A}})>\frac{1}{2\lambda\eta}
and
\sigma_{\min}(\check{\mathbf{A}})>\frac{1}{2\lambda\eta}
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{X}\|}{(2\lambda\eta)^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\|}{2(\lambda\eta)^{3}}.

(138)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\|=

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\sum_{j}\Delta_{\ell,ii}^{2}}\overset{(a)}{\leq}~{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha}\|_{2}\overset{(b)}{\leq}~{\rm PolyLog}(n)\frac{|\dot{\ell}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha})|\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}}{2\lambda\eta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|}{2\eta\lambda}

(139)

under the event
{\cal A}_{L}\cap{\cal A}_{s}
. Here Inequality (a) is a result of (
10
) in Assumption A4. Note that we are using the same notation for all the terms that are polynomial functions of
\log(n)
. To obtain Inequality (b) we have used Part 2 of Lemma
3
.
Finally,

\displaystyle\|\check{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}=\|\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}\|\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}}{\sigma_{\min}(\check{\mathbf{A}})}\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}}{2\lambda\eta}.

(140)

To finish our bound for (
137
) we have to bound
\|{\check{\mathbf{v}}}\|_{\infty}
. Note that

{\check{\mathbf{v}}}=\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}.

The main difficulty in bounding this term is that due to the dependence of
{\cal B}^{+}
on
\mathbf{x}_{i}
it is hard to characterize the distribution of the elements of
{\check{\mathbf{v}}}
. To remove this dependency we again want to use the union bound on the different choices of
{\cal B}^{+}
. Towards this goal, suppose that for a fixed set
{\cal T}
of size larger than
p-d_{n}
we define:

\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}=\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal T}},

where

\displaystyle\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle={\rm diag}\left[\lambda\left\{{\ddot{r}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha})\right\}\right]+\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}^{\top}{\rm diag}\left[\ddot{\ell}_{/i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i})\right]\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}.

Note that the distribution of
\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}
given
\mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i}
is
N\left(0,\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}^{-2}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}^{\top}\right)
. Furthermore, we have

\|\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}\check{\check{\mathbf{A}}}^{-2}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal T}}^{\top}\|\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{X}_{/i}\mathbf{X}_{/i}^{\top}\|}{4\eta^{2}\lambda^{2}}.

Hence, using Corollary
5.1

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i}\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}_{i}>\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{X}_{/i}\mathbf{X}_{/i}^{\top}\|}{n\lambda\eta}\log 2n}+t\ |\ \mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\rm e}^{-\frac{4n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}t^{2}}{\|\mathbf{X}_{/i}\mathbf{X}_{/i}^{\top}\|}}.

(142)

It is straightforward to check that

\max_{i}\|\mathbf{X}_{/i}\mathbf{X}_{/i}^{\top}\|\leq\|\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\|.

Furthermore, according to Lemma
19
with probability larger than
{\rm e}^{-p}
we have

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|\geq(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max})\leq{\rm e}^{-p}.

(143)

Define

\gamma_{4}(n):=\sqrt{\frac{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}{n\lambda\eta}\log 2n},

(144)

and let the event
{\cal E}
denote the event that
\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|\leq(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}
. Then, from (
143
) and (
142
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i}\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}_{i}>\gamma_{4}(n)+t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i}\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}_{i}>\gamma_{4}(n)+t,{\cal E}\right)+\mathbb{P}({\cal E}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{4n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}t^{2}}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}.

(145)

Note that we expect
\rho_{\max}=O(\frac{1}{p})
. Hence,
\gamma_{4}(n)
is expected to be
O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)
.
So far, we have only considered a bound for
\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}
in which a fixed set
{\cal T}
is only considered, despite the fact that the quantity we are interested in is
\|\check{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\infty}
in which
{\cal T}
is replaced with set
{\cal B}^{+}
. To resolve the issue we use the union bound. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{{\cal T}:|{\cal T}|>p-d_{n}}\max_{i}\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}_{i}>\gamma_{4}(n)+t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{{\cal T}:|{\cal T}|>p-d_{n}}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i}\check{\check{\mathbf{v}}}_{i}>\gamma_{4}(n)+t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~d_{n}{p\choose p-d_{n}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}t^{2}}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\rm e}^{2d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{4n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}t^{2}}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right).

(146)

Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}>\gamma_{4}(n)+t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\rm e}^{2d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}t^{2}}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right).

(147)

Setting
t=\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sqrt{\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{n}}
and combining this equation with (
137
), (
2
), (
2
), and (
140
), we conclude that if we define

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}(n)

\displaystyle:=\frac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}}{2\lambda\eta}\left(\gamma_{4}(n)+\dfrac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sqrt{\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|}{2\lambda\eta}\frac{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|^{2}\|\mathbf{X}\|}{4\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|c_{3}(n)c_{4}(n)}{4\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}}\right),

(148)

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}\bm{\delta}_{A^{-1}}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|>\gamma_{5}(n))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~q_{n}+\check{q}_{n}+{\rm e}^{2d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right).

(149)

As discussed before, we have
\gamma_{4}(n)=O(\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}})
. Also, all the norms
\|\mathbf{X}\|,\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|,\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|
are
O_{P}(1)
, hence we expect
\gamma_{5}(n)
to go to zero at the rate
\sqrt{d_{n}{{\rm PolyLog}}(n)/n}
. This heuristic argument will be be made rigorous later.

3.

\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\delta}_{B}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}
:

Using a similar argument as the one presented in (
137
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\delta}_{B}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{-}}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{-}}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}{\check{\mathbf{v}}}=\check{\mathbf{u}}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell}{\check{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{\sum_{i}\bm{\Delta}_{\ell,ii}^{2}}\|\check{\mathbf{u}}\|(\max_{i}|\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\check{\mathbf{v}}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(150)

In the above equations we have used

\check{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}:=\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{-}},

and

{\check{\mathbf{v}}}=\mathbf{X}_{/i,{\cal B}^{+}}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}.

According to (
2
) and (
2
) we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{j}\Delta_{\ell,ii}^{2}}\leq\frac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}}{2\lambda\eta},

(151)

with probability larger than
1-q_{n}-\check{q}_{n}
, and

\displaystyle\|\check{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{X}\|\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}}{2\lambda\eta},

(152)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i}\check{\mathbf{v}}_{i}>\gamma_{5}(n)+t\right)\leq

\displaystyle~{\rm e}^{2d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{4n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}t^{2}}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right).

(153)

Hence, the only remaining term to bound is
\|\check{\mathbf{u}}\|
. It is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\|\check{\mathbf{u}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|\|\mathbf{X}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\tilde{\mathbf{A}})}\leq\frac{\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|\|\mathbf{X}\|}{2\lambda\eta}.

(154)

Hence, combining (
3
), (
151
), (
152
), (
153
), and (
154
) we will have that if we set
t=\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sqrt{\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{n}}
, and define

\displaystyle\gamma_{6}(n):=

\displaystyle~\frac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{3}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\|}{(2\lambda\eta)^{3}}\left(\gamma_{5}(n)+\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sqrt{\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{n}}\right),

(155)

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\bm{\delta}_{B}^{\top}\check{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}|>\gamma_{6}(n))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~q_{n}+\check{q}_{n}+{\rm e}^{2s\log\frac{ep}{s}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+2{\rm e}^{-\frac{4d_{n}\log^{2}p}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right).

(156)

Plugging in the bounds from equations (
1
),(
3
) and similarly bounding the remaining terms in (
5.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}\tilde{\mathbf{B}}_{2}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\gamma_{5}(n)+2\gamma_{6}(n)+\dfrac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{p-d_{n}}+d_{n}\gamma_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{p-d_{n}}+\dfrac{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}d_{n}\rho^{2}_{\max}}{4\eta^{2}\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle~+\frac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{3}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\omega^{s}}{4\lambda^{3}\eta^{3}}\left(1+\dfrac{\omega^{s}}{2\lambda\eta}+\frac{\omega^{s}\|\mathbf{X}\|{\rm PolyLog}(n)}{8\lambda^{3}\eta^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}{n\lambda\eta}\log 2n}+\dfrac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sqrt{\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{n}}\right).

with probability at least

\displaystyle 1-{\rm e}^{d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left(2{\rm e}^{-c\left(\frac{d_{n}\log^{4}p}{\gamma_{3}^{2}}\wedge\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{\gamma_{3}}\right)}\right)-q_{n}-\check{q}_{n}-{\rm e}^{-p}-2{\rm e}^{2d_{n}\log\frac{ep}{d_{n}}}\left({\rm e}^{-p}+4{\rm e}^{-\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~1-{\rm e}^{-d_{n}\log p}-q_{n}-\check{q}_{n}

provided
{\rm e}\leq d_{n}\leq\frac{p}{C}
for a sufficiently large constant
C>0
, where we use the definition of
\gamma_{3}
,
\gamma_{4}
,
\gamma_{5}
,
\gamma_{6}
along with the fact that
\rho_{\max}=O(p^{-1})
.

We now return to equations (
109
) and (
120
), along with the bound derived immediately above, to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}(\omega^{s})^{2}\left(\dfrac{\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha)}{\lambda\eta}+(\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha))^{2}+(\gamma_{1}^{s}(\alpha))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle~+\dfrac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{p-d_{n}}+\dfrac{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}d_{n}\rho^{2}_{\max}}{4\eta^{2}\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle~+\frac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{3}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\omega^{s}}{4\lambda^{3}\eta^{3}}\left(1+\dfrac{\omega^{s}}{2\lambda\eta}+\frac{\omega^{s}\|\mathbf{X}\|{\rm PolyLog}(n)}{8\lambda^{3}\eta^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}}{n\lambda\eta}\log 2n}+\dfrac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sqrt{\frac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{n}}\right)

(157)

with probability at least
1-p^{-d_{n}}-q_{n}-\check{q}_{n}
. We now simplify the above bound by plugging in high probability bounds on the respective parameters. To this end, we first state:

•

By Assumption A1,
\rho_{\max}=\sigma_{\max}(\Sigma)\leq C_{X}/p
.

•

By Lemmas
17
and
19
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\leq 2\sqrt{C_{X}}\text{ and }\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|\leq(\sqrt{\gamma_{0}}+3)^{2}C_{X}

with probability at least
1-(n+1){\rm e}^{-p/2}
.

•

Next,

\displaystyle\omega_{s}=

\displaystyle~\|\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\ddot{\ell}(t\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}+(1-t)\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~(\sqrt{n}+3\sqrt{p})^{2}\rho_{\max}{\rm PolyLog}(n)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C_{X}(\sqrt{\gamma_{0}}+3)^{2}{\rm PolyLog}(n)

with probability at least
1-{\rm e}^{-p}
, by Lemma
19
and Assumption A4.

•

By equation (
108
), we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{s}(\alpha)\leq\dfrac{1}{p}

provided
\alpha\geq Cp/\lambda^{2}\eta(1-\eta)\kappa_{0}
for sufficiently large
p
and a sufficiently large constant
C>0
.

•

By equation (
119
) and the above inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{s}(\alpha)\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{16}{\lambda\alpha(1-\eta)\kappa_{0}}\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\lambda\eta}(C_{X})^{2}(\sqrt{\gamma_{0}}+3)^{4}{\rm PolyLog}(n)\right)\leq~\dfrac{1}{p}

provided
\alpha\geq Cp{\rm PolyLog}(n)/\lambda^{2}\eta(1-\eta)\kappa_{0}
for sufficiently large
p
and a sufficiently large constant
C>0
.

Plugging in all these bounds into (
5.6
) we conclude that there is a sufficiently large numerical constant
C>0
, depending only on
\gamma_{0}
,
C_{X}
and such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i,{\cal B}_{1,+}}\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{{\rm PolyLog}(n)}{\lambda^{3}\eta^{3}(1\wedge\lambda\eta)^{3}}\sqrt{\dfrac{d_{n}}{n\lambda\eta}}+\dfrac{Cd_{n}}{n\lambda^{2}\eta^{2}}+\dfrac{d_{n}\log^{2}p}{p-d_{n}}+\dfrac{C}{p\lambda\eta}

with probability at least
1-(n+1){\rm e}^{-\frac{p}{2}}-p^{-d_{n}}-q_{n}-\check{q}_{n}
. ∎

5.7
Proof of Lemma
6

We prove the lemma only for
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
, because the same proof and constants apply to the leave-one-out estimators
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}
.

Recall that in Lemmas
5
,
7
and
8
,
\widehat{\mu}
is the empirical distribution of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
,
\mu^{*}
is the distribution of
\frac{b_{*}}{b_{*}+2\lambda\eta\tau_{*}}{\rm soft}(\tau_{*}Z+\Theta,\frac{\lambda(1-\eta)\tau_{*}}{b_{*}})
with
(\Theta,Z)\sim\frac{1}{p}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{p}\delta_{\beta^{*}_{k}}\otimes N(0,1)
, and
(b_{*},\tau_{*})
is the unique solution of the equations (
50
) and (
51
).
Note that by Lemma
24
, there exists
0<b_{\min}<b_{\max}
and
\sigma<\tau_{\max}
depending only on model parameters such that
\sigma<\tau_{*}<\tau_{\max}
and
b_{\min}<b_{*}<b_{\max}
.

Let us first define a smoothed indicator function as follows:

\displaystyle h_{\zeta}(x)=\begin{cases}0&,\lvert x\rvert\geq\kappa_{1}+\zeta\\
1-\dfrac{\lvert x\rvert-\kappa_{1}}{\zeta}&,\kappa_{1}\leq\lvert x\rvert<\kappa_{1}+\zeta\\
1&,\lvert x\rvert<\kappa_{1}\end{cases}

(158)

Note that
h_{\zeta}
is
\frac{1}{\zeta}
-Lipschitz, and
\mathbbm{1}_{[-\kappa_{1},\kappa_{1}]}\leq h_{\zeta}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-\kappa_{1}-\zeta,\kappa_{1}+\zeta]}
. We then have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\#\{k:0<\lvert\widehat{\beta}_{k}\rvert\leq\kappa_{1}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\widehat{\mu}([-\kappa_{1},\kappa_{1}])-\widehat{\mu}(\{0\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int\mathbbm{1}_{[-\kappa_{1},\kappa_{1}]}(x)d\widehat{\mu}(x)-\widehat{\mu}(\{0\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int h_{\zeta}(x)d\widehat{\mu}(x)-\widehat{\mu}(\{0\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int h_{\zeta}(x)d\mu_{*}(x)+\left\lvert\int h_{\zeta}(x)d\mu_{*}(x)-\int h_{\zeta}(x)d\widehat{\mu}_{*}(x)\right\rvert-\widehat{\mu}(\{0\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mu_{*}([-\kappa_{1}-\zeta,\kappa_{1}+\zeta])-\mu_{*}(\{0\})+\left\lvert\int h_{\zeta}(x)d\mu_{*}(x)-\int h_{\zeta}(x)d\widehat{\mu}_{*}(x)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\mu_{*}(\{0\})-\widehat{\mu}(\{0\})\right\rvert.

(159)

We now aim to obtain upper bounds for the final three terms in (
5.7
). First note that
0
is the unique discontinuity of
\mu_{*}
, therefore

\displaystyle\mu_{*}([-\kappa_{1}-\zeta,\kappa_{1}+\zeta])-\mu_{*}(\{0\})\leq 2(\kappa_{1}+\zeta)\max_{x\neq 0}f_{*}(x)

where
f_{*}
is the density of the absolutely continuous part of
\mu_{*}
w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure. It can be verified directly via definition that

\displaystyle f_{*}(x)=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{p}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{*}}+\frac{2\lambda\eta}{b_{*}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{p}\phi\left(x\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{*}}+\frac{2\lambda\eta}{b_{*}}\right)+{\rm sign}(x)\frac{\lambda(1-\eta)}{b_{*}}-\frac{\beta_{k}^{*}}{\tau_{*}}\right)

where
\phi(\cdot)
is the standard Gaussian density, and therefore

\displaystyle f_{*}(x)\leq

\displaystyle~(\sqrt{2\pi})^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{*}}+\frac{2\lambda\eta}{b_{*}}\right)\leq~(\sqrt{2\pi})^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma}+\frac{2\lambda_{max}}{b_{min}}\right)=:C_{f}

So we have

\mu_{*}([-\kappa_{1}-\zeta,\kappa_{1}+\zeta])-\mu_{*}(\{0\})\leq 2C_{f}(\kappa_{1}+\zeta).

(160)

To bound the term
\left\lvert\int h_{\zeta}(x)\mu_{*}(x)-\int h_{\zeta}(x)\widehat{\mu}_{*}(x)\right\rvert
in (
5.7
) we use the following approach. First, note that
h_{\zeta}
is
1/\zeta
-Lipschitz. Thus,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int h_{\zeta}(x)\mu_{*}(x)-\int h_{\zeta}(x)\widehat{\mu}_{*}(x)\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle~\dfrac{1}{\zeta}\sup_{g\in L_{1}}\left\lvert\int g(x)d\mu_{*}(x)-\int g(x)d\widehat{\mu}_{*}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~W_{1}(\widehat{\mu},\mu_{*})/\zeta\leq W_{2}(\widehat{\mu},\mu_{*})/\zeta.

(161)

In these equations,
W_{q}
denotes the Wasserstein-q distance between two measures for
q=1,2
. The last inequality uses monotonicity of
W_{q}
in
q
: for
0<p<q
:

\displaystyle W_{p}(\mu,\nu):=\inf_{X\sim\mu,Y\sim\nu}\lVert X-Y\rVert_{{\cal L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{X\sim\mu,Y\sim\nu}\lVert X-Y\rVert_{{\cal L}_{q}}=:W_{q}(\mu,\nu).

Furthermore, by Lemma
5
, for any
0<\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
, we have

W_{2}(\widehat{\mu},\mu_{*})/\zeta\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}/\zeta

(162)

with probability at least
1-C_{1}\varepsilon^{-2}{\rm e}^{-c_{1}p\varepsilon^{3}(\log\varepsilon)^{-2}}
, for some constants
C_{1},c_{1}
. Combining (
5.7
) and (
163
) we conclude that

\left\lvert\int h_{\zeta}(x)\mu_{*}(x)-\int h_{\zeta}(x)\widehat{\mu}_{*}(x)\right\rvert\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}/\zeta

(163)

with probability at least
1-C_{1}\varepsilon^{-2}{\rm e}^{-c_{1}p\varepsilon^{3}(\log\varepsilon)^{-2}}
.

For the last term of (
5.7
) we use Lemma
8
to get:

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mu}(0)-\mu_{*}(0)|=|\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|_{0}-s_{*}|<\varepsilon^{\prime}

(164)

with probability at least
1-C_{2}\varepsilon^{\prime-6}{\rm e}^{-c_{2}p\varepsilon^{\prime 6}}
.

Using (
5.7
), (
160
), (
163
), and (
164
) together we have

\frac{1}{p}\left\lvert\{k:0<\lvert\widehat{\beta}_{k}\rvert\leq\kappa_{1}\}\right\rvert\leq 2C_{f}(\kappa_{1}+\zeta)+\sqrt{\varepsilon}/\zeta+\varepsilon^{\prime}

with probability at least
1-C_{1}\varepsilon^{-2}{\rm e}^{-c_{1}p\varepsilon^{3}(\log\varepsilon)^{-2}}-C_{2}\varepsilon^{\prime-6}{\rm e}^{-c_{2}p\varepsilon^{\prime 6}}
.
We observe first that the bound is minimized at
\zeta=(2C_{f})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
without changing the probability.

Next, we set
\varepsilon=2(c_{1}p)^{-\frac{1}{3}}\log p
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}=3^{\frac{1}{3}}(c_{2}p)^{-\frac{1}{6}}(\log p)^{\frac{1}{6}}
to get:

\displaystyle~\left\lvert\{k:0<\lvert\widehat{\beta}_{k}\rvert\leq\kappa_{1}\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2C_{f}p\kappa_{1}+C_{3}p^{\frac{11}{12}}(\log p)^{\frac{1}{4}}+C_{4}p^{\frac{5}{6}}(\log p)^{\frac{1}{6}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Cp\kappa_{1}+Cp^{\frac{11}{12}}(\log p)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Cp^{\frac{11}{12}}(\log p)^{\frac{1}{4}}

with probability at least
1-C^{\prime}p^{-7}
, for sufficiently large
p
and some constants
C,C^{\prime}
, provided
\kappa_{1}=o(p^{-\frac{1}{12}}(\log p)^{\frac{1}{4}})
By an identical argument, an analogous statement holds for
\widehat{\beta}_{/i,k}
. The proof is completed by a union bound over
1\leq i\leq n
. ∎

5.8
Proof of Proposition
3.1

Without loss of generality we assume
C=1
. The first step is to show that
\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}=O_{p}({\rm PolyLog}(n))
.
Throughout this proof we use the following notations:

\displaystyle h(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ell(y_{i};\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\beta})+\lambda(1-\eta)\sum_{i=1}^{p}|\beta_{i}|+\lambda\eta\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta},

\displaystyle h_{/\penalty i}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}\ell(y_{i};\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\beta})+\lambda(1-\eta)\sum_{i=1}^{p}|\beta_{i}|+\lambda\eta\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta},

\displaystyle h_{\alpha}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\ell(y_{i};\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\beta})+\lambda r_{\alpha}(\bm{\beta}),

\displaystyle h_{\alpha,/\penalty i}(\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}\ell(y_{i};\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\beta})+\lambda r_{\alpha}(\bm{\beta}),

(165)

where
r_{\alpha}(\cdot)
is define in (
20
).

(a)

First note that for all
i
,

\displaystyle\lambda\eta\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~\sum_{j}\ell(y_{j}|\mathbf{x}_{j}\top\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i})+\lambda(1-\eta)\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|_{1}+\lambda\eta\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|_{2}^{2}\leq~\sum_{j}\ell(y_{j}|0),

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
h_{/\penalty i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i})\leq h_{/\penalty i}(\mathbf{0})
.
Under the event that
\forall i,|y_{i}|\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n)
which holds with probability at least
1-nq_{n}^{(y)}
according to the assumptions, we have

\displaystyle\max_{i}\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sum_{j}\ell(y_{j}|0)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}\sum_{j}(|y_{j}|^{m}+1)\leq n({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle(\max_{i}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}|>t)\leq\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{P}(|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}|>t|\mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{P}(|N(0,\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|^{2}}{n})|>t|\mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\mathbb{E}\mathbb{P}(|N(0,1)|>\frac{t\sqrt{n}}{\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|}|\mathbf{X}_{/i},\mathbf{y}_{/i})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{i}\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|^{2}>n({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m})

\displaystyle+n\mathbb{P}\left(|N(0,1)|>\frac{t}{({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m/2}}\right).

(166)

Let
t=({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m/2}\cdot 2\sqrt{\log(n)}:={\rm PolyLog}(n)
. Then, we can use (
(a)
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle(\max_{i}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}|>{{\rm PolyLog}}(n))

\displaystyle\leq nq_{n}^{(y)}+2ne^{-\frac{1}{2}(2\sqrt{\log(n)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq nq_{n}^{(y)}+\frac{2}{n}.

(167)

With a similar strategy we can also prove that for any
\alpha
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\max_{i}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\alpha}_{/i}|>{{\rm PolyLog}}(n))\leq nq_{n}^{(y)}+\frac{2}{n}.

(168)

(b)

Now we set
\alpha=1
and work within the event
\Xi
under which all the following hold:

(a)

\max_{i}|y_{i}|\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n)
,

(b)

\max_{i}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}|\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n)
,

(c)

\max_{i}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}|\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n)
.

(d)

\max_{i}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\leq 2\sqrt{C_{X}}
.

Note that by combining the assumption of theorem with (
(a)
), (
168
), and Lemma
17
we have

\mathbb{P}(\Xi)\geq 1-3nq_{n}^{(y)}-\frac{4}{n}-ne^{-p/2}.

Under
\Xi
we have

\displaystyle\dot{\ell}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})

\displaystyle=\dot{\ell}_{i}(y_{i}|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1+({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m}+({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m}

\displaystyle={\rm PolyLog}(n).

Next, consider the following first order optimality conditions for
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}
and
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\mathbf{x}_{j}\dot{\ell}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1})+\lambda\dot{r}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1})=0,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\neq i}\mathbf{x}_{j}\dot{\ell}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})+\lambda\dot{r}_{\alpha}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})=0

By subtracting the two equations and using mean value theorem we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}^{\top}\diag(\ddot{\ell}_{j}(\bm{\xi}))\mathbf{X}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})+\lambda\diag(\ddot{r}_{\alpha}(\bm{\xi}))(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{x}_{i}\dot{\ell}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}),

(169)

where
\bm{\xi}
and
\tilde{\bm{\xi}}
are convex combinations of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}
and
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbf{X}^{\top}\diag(\ddot{\ell}_{j}(\bm{\xi}))\mathbf{X}+\lambda\diag(\ddot{r}_{\alpha}(\tilde{\bm{\xi}}))\right]^{-1}\mathbf{x}_{i}\dot{\ell}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}).

Hence, it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda\eta}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\||\dot{\ell}_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\lambda\eta}2\sqrt{C_{X}}{\rm PolyLog}(n)

\displaystyle={\rm PolyLog}(n)

(170)

given that
\frac{1}{\lambda\eta}=O({\rm PolyLog}(n))
. We can now use (
(b)
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}\|+\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{1}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}\|+\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}^{1}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n)+2\sqrt{\frac{4p\log(2)}{\eta}}.

(171)

Recall that

{\cal D}:=\cup_{1\leq i\leq n,t\in[0,1]}{\cal B}(t\widehat{\bm{\beta}}+(1-t)\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i},\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\epsilon}}),

where
{\cal B}(x,r)
is the ball with center
x
and radius
r
. Hence we can conclude that
\forall i,\forall\mathbf{v}\in{\cal D}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{v}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|\leq

\displaystyle~\|\mathbf{v}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}\|+\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|\leq~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\epsilon}}+{\rm PolyLog}(n)+8\sqrt{\frac{p\log(2)}{\eta}},

(172)

and

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{v}|\leq

\displaystyle~|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}|+|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{v}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\rm PolyLog}(n)+\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|\|\mathbf{v}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{/i}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\rm PolyLog}(n)+2\sqrt{C_{X}}(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{\epsilon}}+2{\rm PolyLog}(n))+8\sqrt{\frac{4C_{x}p\log(2)}{\eta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\rm PolyLog}(n).

Hence,

\displaystyle\dot{\ell}_{i}(\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle\leq 1+|y_{i}|^{m}+|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{v}|^{m}

\displaystyle\leq 1+({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m}+({\rm PolyLog}(n))^{m}

\displaystyle={\rm PolyLog}(n)

Using the same arguments one can show that

\displaystyle\ddot{\ell}_{i}(\mathbf{v})\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n);\dddot{\ell}_{i}(\mathbf{v})\leq{\rm PolyLog}(n).

This completes the proof. ∎

6
Conclusion

In this paper, we have introduced a novel theoretical framework that offers error bounds for the disparity between the computationally intensive leave-one-out risk estimate (LO) and its more computationally efficient approximation (ALO). We focus in a regime where
n/p
and SNR remain fixed and bounded regardless of how large
n
and
p
growth. For problems in the generalized linear model family such as linear Gaussian, Poisson and logistic, we bound the error between ALO and LO in terms of intuitive metrics such as perturbation size of leave-
i
-out active sets. Next, for least squares problems with elastic-net regularization, we show that these purturbations scales sub-linearly with
n
and
p
, and consequently, the difference
|{\rm ALO-LO}|
approaches zero as
n,p\rightarrow\infty
.

A
Study of the Elastic Net Estimator

A.1
Objective

As mentioned in Section 3 of the main part of the paper, our proofs use concentration of measure results for the empirical distribution of the regression coefficeints and the subgradient vector. Some of the results we use in our paper are due to
Miolane and Montanari 2021
. However, the results of
Miolane and Montanari 2021
are stated for the LASSO estimator and not the elastic-net. Hence, the results we require are different from those presented in
Miolane and Montanari 2021
. However, the changes do not constitute significant advancements that would warrant the derivation of elastic-net results as a major contribution. As a result, we have included these findings in a dedicated section, which will serve as an online appendix to our paper. This section will not be part of the formal submission to a journal but is included for the sake of completeness.

Throughout this appendix we will mainly focus on Theorem 3.1, Theorem E.5 and Theorem F.1 of
Miolane and Montanari 2021
. For the sake of brevity we do not present the proof with every details, since the proof technique is very similar to that of
Miolane and Montanari 2021
. We only focus on the differences.
Consider the elastic net problem:

\widehat{\bm{\beta}}:=\underset{\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\argmin}~{\cal L}(\bm{\beta})=\underset{\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\argmin}~\frac{1}{2n}\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\bm{\beta}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)+\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\bm{\beta}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right).

Note that to simplify the proof we have subtracted
\|\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{1}
and
\|\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
and used a different scaling than the one presented in the paper. However, as is obvious, these changes do not have any effect on
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
. Furthermore, the definition here is slightly different from the one in the main paper, where the LASSO penalty is
\lambda(1-\eta)
, and the ridge penalty is
\lambda\eta
. The difference is not substantial and is only for notational brevity.
Let
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}=\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}
denote the estimation error. Using the assumption
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}+\sigma\mathbf{z}
, the problem can be written as:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{w}}

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\argmin}\;{\cal C}(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\argmin}\frac{1}{2n}\lVert\sigma\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)+\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right).

(173)

We assume there exist
0<\lambda_{\min}<\lambda_{\max}
,
\eta_{\max}>0
such that
\lambda\in[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]
and
\eta\in(0,\eta_{\max}]
.

Suppose that we are interested in the asymptotic distribution of the elements of
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}
. The main approach that can help us in characterizing the distribution is the convex Gaussian minimax theorem that we would like to introduce briefly next.

A.2
Convex Gaussian Minimax Theorem

In this appendix, we require a few notations that we aim to introduce in this section.
Consider the mean square error of the elastic net, i.e.
\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}
. It has been shown in
Maleki 2010
;
Donoho et al. 2011b
;
Donoho et al. 2009b
;
Thrampoulidis et al. 2015
;
Thrampoulidis et al. 2018
that under the asymptotic settings
n/p\rightarrow\delta
and under Assumptions B1-B5 of our paper, the mean square error converges to
\delta(\tau_{*}^{2}-\sigma^{2})
, where
\tau_{*}^{2}
is a saddle point of the following function:

\displaystyle\psi(\tau,b)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\tau}+\tau\right)\frac{b}{2}-\frac{b^{2}}{2}+\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\min_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left\{\frac{b}{2\tau}\lVert\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}^{2}-b\mathbf{g}^{\top}\mathbf{w}+\lambda\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+\eta\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right).

(174)

Define

\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}(\tau,b):=\frac{b}{b+2\eta\tau}{\rm soft}\left(\tau\mathbf{g}+\bm{\beta}^{*},\frac{\lambda\tau}{b}\right)-\bm{\beta}^{*}

where
\mathbf{g}\sim N(0,I_{p})
and

[{\rm soft}(\mathbf{x},r)]_{i}=((|x_{i}|-r)_{+}\sgn(x_{i}))_{i=1}^{p}

is the element-wise soft thresholding function. Let
(\tau_{*},b_{*})
denote the unique saddle point of
\psi(\tau,b)
. Define

\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}:=\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}(\tau^{*},b^{*})

The following theorems state that the saddle point exists, is unique, and is bounded.

Lemma 23
.

\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\psi(\tau,b)
is achieved at a unique couple
(\tau_{*},b_{*})
which is also the unique solution of the following system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\tau^{2}=\sigma^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2},\\
b=\tau-\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}g^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}.\end{array}\right.

Lemma 24
.

There exist
b_{min}>0
,
\tau_{max}>0
,
b_{max}>0
that depend only on
\delta,\sigma,\xi
such that
b_{min}\leq b_{*}\leq b_{max}
and
\sigma<\tau_{*}\leq\tau_{max}

The proof of these theorems are postponed to Section
A.4
.

The convex Gaussian minimax framework makes the connection between
\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\tau_{*}^{2}
through a few steps that we clarify below. Again consider the optimization problem:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{w}}

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\argmin}\;{\cal C}(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\argmin}\frac{1}{2n}\lVert\sigma\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)+\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right).

Note that we can rewite this optimization problem as the following saddle point problem using dual representation of the
l_{2}
norm:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{w}}:=\arg\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}{\min}\max_{\mathbf{u}}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}-\frac{1}{2n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{u}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\sigma\mathbf{z}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)+\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right).

According to the Convex Gaussian minimax framework we can construct a simpler auxilary saddle point problem that can provide useful information about
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}
. To clarify this point, define

\displaystyle\Phi(X)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}{\min}\max_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{w}-\frac{1}{2n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{u}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\sigma\mathbf{z}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)

(177)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{w}}{\min}\max_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{S}_{u}}\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\mathbf{u}^{\top}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}},-\mathbf{z})\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{w}\\
-\sqrt{n}\sigma\end{pmatrix}-\frac{1}{2n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{u}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)

(178)

where
{\cal S}_{w}
and
{\cal S}_{u}
are two convex, compact sets. Note that
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}},-\mathbf{z})
is a matrix with i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries. Define the following auxillary optimization problem:

\displaystyle\phi(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})

(179)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{w}\in{\cal S}_{w}}{\min}\max_{\mathbf{u}\in{\cal S}_{u}}\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{h}^{\top}\mathbf{u}-\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\|\mathbf{u}\|\mathbf{g}^{\top}\mathbf{w}+\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{u}\|g^{\prime}\sigma-\frac{1}{2n}\mathbf{u}^{\top}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)+\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right)

where
\mathbf{h}\sim N(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})
,
\mathbf{g}\sim N(0,\mathbb{I}_{p})
,
g^{\prime}\sim N(0,1)
, and all of them are independent and also independent of
\mathbf{z}
.

According to the Gaussian minimax theorem, i.e. Theorem 3 of
Thrampoulidis et al. 2015
, when
{\cal S}_{w}
and
{\cal S}_{u}
are convex compact sets we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Phi(X)<t)\leq 2\mathbb{P}(\phi(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})<t)

(180)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\Phi(X)>t)\leq 2\mathbb{P}(\phi(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})>t)

(181)

Using this theorem, and by using a proper choice for
{\cal S}_{w}
we can analyze certain properties of
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}
through the minimizer of the easier auxillary function:

\displaystyle L(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\lVert\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}\frac{\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{g}^{\top}\mathbf{w}+\frac{g^{\prime}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\right)_{+}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right)

(182)

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta}{n}\left(\lVert\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right).

Note that
L(\mathbf{w})
is obtained from
\phi(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})
, when the supremum with respect to
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
is calculated.

Let us explain how
L(\mathbf{w})
can be connected with the scaler saddle point problem presented in (
A.2
). Actually
\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})
concentrates around
\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\psi(\tau,b)
. Below is a heuristic argument. For simplicity we denote
r(\mathbf{w})=\lambda(\|\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{1}-\|\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{1})+\eta(\|\mathbf{w}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}-\|\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2})

First notice that
\frac{\|\mathbf{h}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}
concentrates around 1, and
\frac{g^{\prime}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}
concentrates around 0, so heuristically, they can be removed from the expression which lead to:

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle\approx\min_{\mathbf{w}}\frac{1}{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{g}^{\top}\mathbf{w}\right)_{+}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}r(\mathbf{w})

Next we use the fact that
a_{+}^{2}=\max_{b\geq 0}ab-\frac{1}{2}b^{2}
and
\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}=\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\frac{\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}{2\tau}+\frac{\tau}{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle\approx\min_{\mathbf{w}}\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\left(\frac{\frac{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}{2\tau}+\frac{\tau}{2}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{g}^{\top}\mathbf{w}\right)b-\frac{1}{2}b^{2}+\frac{1}{n}r(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\tau}+\tau\right)\frac{b}{2}-\frac{1}{2}b^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\min_{\mathbf{w}}\left\{\frac{b}{2\tau}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbf{g}^{\top}\mathbf{w}+r(\mathbf{w})\right\}

\displaystyle:=\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}F(\tau,b,\mathbf{w})

Step (a) requires some delicate arguments which are omitted here. They intend to prove that the minimum ad maximum operation are interchangeable. Finally, one can show that
\max\limits_{b\geq 0}\min\limits_{\tau\geq\sigma}F(\tau,b,\mathbf{w})
concentrates around

\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\mathbb{E}F(\tau,b,\mathbf{w})=\max_{b\geq 0}\min_{\tau\geq\sigma}\psi(\tau,b)=\psi(\tau^{*},b^{*}).

Therefore,
\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})
concentrates around
\psi(b_{*},\tau_{*})
.

Now we will use the above arguments for a specific choice of
\mathcal{S}_{w}
that allows us to obtain the mean square error of
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}
. For this purpose we define
\mathcal{S}_{w}={\cal D}(\varepsilon):=\{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\frac{1}{p}\lVert\mathbf{w}-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}>\varepsilon\}
. We first mention the roadmap of the proof to help the readers navigate through the following theorems:

•

We first show that the minimizer of
L(\mathbf{w})
is with high probability in a ball of radius
\epsilon^{2}
around
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}
, and hence
\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})
is with high probability very close to
\psi(b_{*},\tau_{*})
. This is done in Lemma
25
, Lemma
26
, and Corollary
A.1
.

•

In the next step we use the minimax theorem to show that the minimizer of
\mathcal{C}(\mathbf{w})
defined in (
173
) is with high probability in the complement of
{\cal D}(\epsilon)
. This is proved in Lemmas
29
and
28
. One can then use Lemma
28
to obtain information about
\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}\|_{2}^{2}
, which is the same as the mean square error of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
.

Our first result aims to connect the minimizer of
L(\mathbf{w})
with
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}
.

Lemma 25
.

There exist

\gamma,C,c>0
depending only on
\Omega
, such that
\forall\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\mathbf{w}\in{\cal D}(\varepsilon)}L(\mathbf{w})<\min_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}L(\mathbf{w})+\gamma\varepsilon\right)\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}e^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

where
{\cal D}(\varepsilon):=\{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\frac{1}{p}\lVert\mathbf{w}-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}>\varepsilon\}
.
∥
∥

∥

Throughout the discussion, the term ‘constants’ refers to quantities that rely only on the following set of model parameters

\Omega:=(\delta,\sigma,\xi,\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max},\eta_{\max})
. Recall that
\delta=n/p
, and
\xi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}
;
\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}
control the scale of
\lambda
, and
\eta_{\max}
controls the scale of
\eta
. We do not assume
\eta
to be bounded away from 0, to be consistent with our main text.

The proof is essentially the same as the proof of Theorem B.1 of
Miolane and Montanari 2021
, up to minor modifications to some constants. Hence, we do not repeat the proof here..

Lemma 26
.

There exist constants
C,c>0
such that for all
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(|L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})-\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})|>\varepsilon\right)\leq Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

Proof.

We only need to prove it for
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
for some small constant
\varepsilon_{0}
depending only on model parmeters
\Omega
. The reason is that the probability is non-increasing in
\varepsilon
so we have a naive bound
Ce^{-cn\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}
for
\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
. This flat bound, combined with the sub-Gaussian bound for small
\varepsilon
, is further bounded by
Ce^{-cn\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}
for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and this is the bound we desire.

Using the fix point equations (
23
) we have the following simplification for
\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})
:

\displaystyle\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})=\frac{1}{2}b_{*}^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{n}\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\frac{\lambda}{n}\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}+\frac{\eta}{n}\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\frac{\eta}{n}\lVert\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}

Define

\widehat{b}^{f}:=\left(\sqrt{\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}\frac{\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{g}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\frac{g^{\prime}\sigma}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

And denote
\widehat{b}^{f}_{+}=\widehat{b}^{f}\rm 1_{\widehat{b}^{f}>0}
, we have

\displaystyle|L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})-\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|(\widehat{b}^{f}_{+})^{2}-b_{*}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{n}\left|\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{\eta}{n}\left|\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right|

(183)

The last two terms are relatively easy to bound. Notice that
\mathbf{g}\to\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}=\frac{b_{*}}{b_{*}+2\eta\tau_{*}}{\rm soft}(\tau_{*}\mathbf{g}+\bm{\beta}^{*},\frac{\lambda\tau_{*}}{b_{*}})-\bm{\beta}^{*}
is
\tau_{\max}
Lipschitz, so

1.

\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{1}
is
Cn^{-1}
sub-Gaussian, because
\mathbf{g}\mapsto\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{1}
is
Cn^{-1/2}
Lipschitz.

2.

\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
is
Cn^{-1}
sub-exponential because
\mathbf{g}\to n^{-1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}
is
Cn^{-1/2}
-Lipschitz. Therefore
n^{-1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}
is
Cn^{-1}
-sub-Gaussian, and hence
\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}
is
Cn^{-1}
sub-exponential.

Therefore for the second and third terms of (
183
), we have the following concentrations. For
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
, there exist constants
C,c>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\lambda}{n}\left|\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}-\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{1}\right|>\varepsilon\right)\leq Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

(184)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\eta}{n}\left|\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}+\bm{\beta}^{*}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right|>\varepsilon\right)\leq Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

(185)

Now for the first term
(\widehat{b}^{f}_{+})^{2}-b_{*}^{2}
, first we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{b}^{f}-b_{*}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left|\left(\sqrt{\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\frac{\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}+\tau_{*}\left(\frac{\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}-1\right)-\frac{1}{n}(\mathbf{g}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}-\mathbb{E}\mathbf{g}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})+\frac{\sigma g^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\right|.

We aim to establish concentration results for each of the four terms that appear in (
A.2
). For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sqrt{\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}\right|\frac{\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}>\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}(\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}>2\sqrt{n})+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sqrt{\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\frac{\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}{n}+\sigma^{2}}\right|>\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Ce^{-cn}+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\left|\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}\right|>\frac{\varepsilon}{b}\cdot 2\sigma\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Ce^{-cn}+Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

for small
\varepsilon
. The second inequality above uses sub-Gaussian concentration of
\lVert\mathbf{h}\rVert_{2}
, and the fact that
\sqrt{x+\sigma^{2}}
is
\frac{1}{2\sigma}
Lipschitz in
x
. To obtain the last inequality first note that
\mathbf{g}\to\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\|
is
\tau_{max}
-Lipschitz. Hence,
\frac{\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}
is
C/n
sub-Gaussian, and
\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\|_{2}^{2}
is
C/n
sub-exponential. We can then use Bernstein inequality of sub-exponential random variables (e.g. Theorem 2.8.1 of
Vershynin 2018
) to establish the last inequaltiy.

This implies that

\mathbb{P}(\widehat{b}^{f}<0)\leq\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{b}^{f}-b_{*}|>b_{*})\leq Ce^{-cn}

and similarly

\mathbb{P}(\widehat{b}^{f}>b_{max}+1)\leq Ce^{-cn}

so

\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{b}^{f}_{+}-b_{*}|>\varepsilon)\leq\mathbb{P}(\widehat{b}^{f}<0)+\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{b}^{f}_{-}b_{*}|>\varepsilon)\leq Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{2}|(\widehat{b}^{f}_{+})^{2}-b_{*}^{2}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(|\widehat{b}^{f}_{+}-b_{*}|\cdot|\widehat{b}^{f}_{+}+b_{*}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\widehat{b}^{f}>b_{max}+1)+\mathbb{P}\left(|\widehat{b}^{f}_{+}-b_{*}|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2(2b_{max}+1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-cn}+Ce^{-c\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

(187)

for small
\varepsilon
. Now inserting (
184
), (
185
), and (
A.2
) back into (
183
) we have our final result

\mathbb{P}(|L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})-\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})|>\varepsilon)\leq Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

for small
\varepsilon
.
∎

Lemma 27
.

For all
R>0
there exists constants
C,c>0
that only depend on
(\Omega,R)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\mathbb{P}(L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})>\min_{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n}R}L(\mathbf{w})+\varepsilon)\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}e^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as that of Proposition B.2 in
Miolane and Montanari 2021
and thus omitted.
∎

We would now like to combine Lemma
25
, Lemma
26
and Lemma
27
to prove the following result:

Corollary A.1
.

There exist
C,c>0
depending only on
\Omega
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{P}(|\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})-\psi(b_{*},\tau_{*})|>\varepsilon)\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}e^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

Proof.

L(\mathbf{w})
is
\frac{2\eta}{n}
-strictly convex and
L(\mathbf{w})\to+\infty
when
\lVert\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}\to+\infty
. Therefore
L(\mathbf{w})
possesses a unique global minimizer
\mathbf{w}^{*}
.

By Lemma
25
, the event
\{\frac{1}{p}\lVert\mathbf{w}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 1\}
has probability at least
1-Ce^{-cn}
. On this event we have

\lVert\mathbf{w}^{*}\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}+\lVert\mathbf{w}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}+\sqrt{p}

Recall that
\frac{\|\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\|}{\sqrt{n}}
is
C/n
sub-Gaussian so
\mathbb{P}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}+1)\geq 1-Ce^{-cn}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{w}^{*}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}+C\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}}+C\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n(\tau_{max}^{2}-\sigma^{2})}+C\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle=C\sqrt{n}

(188)

where the second inequality uses Jensen’s Inequality, and the third uses Lemma
23
.

Let event
A
be the intersection of above events, i.e.
A:=\{\frac{1}{p}\lVert\mathbf{w}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 1,\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\mathbb{E}\lVert\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f}\rVert_{2}+1\}
then
A
has probability at least
1-Ce^{-cn}
. On event
A
we have:

\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})=\min_{\lVert\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}\leq C\sqrt{n}}L(\mathbf{w}).

This means

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})>\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})>\min_{\lVert\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}\leq C\sqrt{n}}L(\mathbf{w})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)+Ce^{-cn}

(189)

So we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})-\psi(b_{*},\tau_{*})|>\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(|\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})-L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(|L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})-\psi(b_{*},\tau_{*})|>\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\mathbb{P}\left(L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})>\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)+Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}+Ce^{-cn}+\mathbb{P}\left(L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})>\min_{\lVert\mathbf{w}\rVert_{2}\leq C\sqrt{n}}L(\mathbf{w})+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}+Ce^{-cn}+Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~Ce^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

for small
\varepsilon
. The second inequality uses Lemma
26
and the fact that
L(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{f})>\min_{\mathbf{w}}L(\mathbf{w})
. The third inequality use (
189
). The penultimate inequality uses Lemma
27
with
R
chosen to be the constant
C
in (
188
). Note that all above constants
C,c
may vary line by line but depend only on model parameters in
\Omega
.
∎

Lemma 28
.

There exists
C,c>0
depending only on
\Omega
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\min_{\mathbf{w}}{\cal C}(\mathbf{w})-\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}e^{-cn\varepsilon^{2}}

Proof.

Using the convex Gaussian minimax theorem stated in (
180
) and Corollary
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\min_{\mathbf{w}}{\cal C}(\mathbf{w})-\psi(\tau_{*},b_{*})\right|\geq\varepsilon\right)
